# 10 System configuration controller (SYSCFG)

## 10.1 SYSCFG main features

The STM32G4 series devices feature a set of configuration registers. The main purposes of the system configuration controller are the following:

- Remapping memory areas
- Managing the external interrupt line connection to the GPIOs
- Managing robustness feature
- Setting CCM RAM write protection and software erase
- Configuring FPU interrupts
- Enabling /disabling I2C Fast-mode Plus driving capability on some I/Os and voltage booster for I/Os analog switches.

## 10.2 SYSCFG registers

### 10.2.1 SYSCFG memory remap register (SYSCFG\_MEMRMP)

This register is used for specific configurations on memory remap.

Address offset: 0x00

Reset value: 0x0000 000X (X is the memory mode selected by the BOOT0 pin and BOOT1 option bit)

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.47px"/><col width="499.28px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">FB_MODE:</span> Flash Bank mode selection</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Flash Bank 1 mapped at 0x0800 0000 (and aliased @0x0000 0000<a id="1" name="RM0440%20SYSCFG_RM0440%20SYSCFG_FTB_1"></a><a href="#RM0440%20SYSCFG_RM0440%20SYSCFG_FTH_1"><sup>1</sup></a>) and Flash Bank 2 <span style="font-size : 9.00pt;">mapped at 0x0804 0000 (and aliased at 0x0008 0000)</span></p><p class="TableIndent">1: Flash Bank2 mapped at 0x0800 0000 (and aliased @0x0000 0000<a href="#XREF_58186__1__When_BFB2_bit_is_set%2C_the_system_memory_remains_al29" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">(1)</a><sup></sup>) and Flash Bank 1 mapped at 0x0804 0000 (and aliased at 0x0008 0000)</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="196.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 2:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MEM_MODE</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">:</span> Memory mapping selection</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits control the memory internal mapping at address 0x0000 0000. These bits are used to select the physical remap by software and so, bypass the BOOT pin and the option bit setting. After reset these bits take the value selected by BOOT0 pin (or option bit nBOOT0) and BOOT1 option bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">000: Main Flash memory mapped at 0x00000000<a href="#XREF_58186__1__When_BFB2_bit_is_set%2C_the_system_memory_remains_al29" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">(1)</a><sup></sup>. </p><p class="TableIndent">001: System Flash memory mapped at 0x00000000. </p><p class="TableIndent">010: FSMC memory. </p><p class="TableIndent">011: SRAM1 mapped at 0x00000000. </p><p class="TableIndent">100: QUADSPI memory mapped at 0x0000 0000</p><p class="TableIndent">101: Reserved </p><p class="TableIndent">111: Reserved </p></td></tr></table><div class="Table_FootNotes"><p class="TableFootnote"><a id="1" name="RM0440%20SYSCFG_RM0440%20SYSCFG_FTH_1"></a><a href="#RM0440%20SYSCFG_RM0440%20SYSCFG_FTB_1">1.</a><a id="al29" name="XREF_58186__1__When_BFB2_bit_is_set%2C_the_system_memory_remains_al29">When BFB2 bit is set, the system memory remains aliased at @0x0000 0000</a></p></div></div>


**Note:**

When the FSMC is remapped at address 0x0000 0000, only the first two regions of Bank 1memory controller (Bank1 NOR/PSRAM 1 and NOR/PSRAM 2) can be remapped. In remap mode, the CPU can access the external memory via ICode bus instead of System bus which boosts up the performance.

### 10.2.2 SYSCFG configuration register 1 (SYSCFG\_CFGR1)

Address offset: 0x04

Reset value: 0x7C00 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.47px"/><col width="499.28px"/><tr height="126.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:26</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FPU_IE[5..0]</span>: Floating Point Unit interrupts enable bits</p><p class="TableBodyL">FPU_IE[5]: Inexact interrupt enable</p><p class="TableBodyL">FPU_IE[4]: Input denormal interrupt enable</p><p class="TableBodyL">FPU_IE[3]: Overflow interrupt enable</p><p class="TableBodyL">FPU_IE[2]: underflow interrupt enable</p><p class="TableBodyL">FPU_IE[1]: Divide-by-zero interrupt enable</p><p class="TableBodyL">FPU_IE[0]: Invalid operation interrupt enable</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 25:24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 23</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2C4_FMP</span>: Fast-mode Plus driving capability activation</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the Fm+ driving mode on I2C4 pins selected through AF selection bits.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Fm+ mode is not enabled on I2C4 pins selected through AF selection bits</p><p class="TableBodyL" style="margin-left : 8.91pt;text-indent : -0.47pt;">1: Fm+ mode is enabled on I2C4 pins selected through AF selection bits.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 22</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2C3_FMP:</span> I2C3 Fast-mode Plus driving capability activation</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the Fm+ driving mode on I2C3 pins selected through AF selection bits.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Fm+ mode is not enabled on I2C3 pins selected through AF selection bits</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Fm+ mode is enabled on I2C3 pins selected through AF selection bits. </p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 21</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2C2_FMP:</span> I2C2 Fast-mode Plus driving capability activation</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the Fm+ driving mode on I2C2 pins selected through AF selection bits.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Fm+ mode is not enabled on I2C2 pins selected through AF selection bits</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Fm+ mode is enabled on I2C2 pins selected through AF selection bits. </p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2C1_FMP:</span> I2C1 Fast-mode Plus driving capability activation</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the Fm+ driving mode on I2C1 pins selected through AF selection bits.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Fm+ mode is not enabled on I2C1 pins selected through AF selection bits</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Fm+ mode is enabled on I2C1 pins selected through AF selection bits. </p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2C_PB9_FMP</span>: Fast-mode Plus (Fm+) driving capability activation on PB9</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the Fm+ driving mode for PB9. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: PB9 pin operates in standard mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Fm+ mode enabled on PB9 pin, and the Speed control is bypassed. </p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2C_PB8_FMP</span>: Fast-mode Plus (Fm+) driving capability activation on PB8</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the Fm+ driving mode for PB8. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: PB8 pin operates in standard mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Fm+ mode enabled on PB8 pin, and the Speed control is bypassed. </p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2C_PB7_FMP</span>: Fast-mode Plus (Fm+) driving capability activation on PB7</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the Fm+ driving mode for PB7. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: PB7 pin operates in standard mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Fm+ mode enabled on PB7 pin, and the Speed control is bypassed. </p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2C_PB6_FMP</span>: Fast-mode Plus (Fm+) driving capability activation on PB6</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the Fm+ driving mode for PB6. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: PB6 pin operates in standard mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Fm+ mode enabled on PB6 pin, and the Speed control is bypassed. </p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">ANASWVDD:</span> GPIO analog switch control voltage selection</p><p class="TableIndent">0: I/O analog switches supplied by VDDA or booster when booster is ON</p><p class="TableIndent">1: I/O analog switches supplied by VDD.</p><p class="TableBodyL">Refer to <a href="#XREF_63725__Table_40__BOOSTEN_and_ANASWVDD_set/reset29" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Table 60</a> for bit 9 setting.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">BOOSTEN:</span> I/O analog switch voltage booster enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: I/O analog switches are supplied by V<sub>DDA</sub> voltage. This is the recommended configuration when using the ADC in high V<sub>DDA</sub> voltage operation.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: I/O analog switches are supplied by a dedicated voltage booster (supplied by V<sub>DD</sub>). This is the recommended configuration when using the ADC in low V<sub>DDA</sub> voltage operation.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr></table></div>


[Table 60](#XREF_63725__Table_40__BOOSTEN_and_ANASWVDD_set/reset29) describes when the bit 9 (ANASWVDD) and the bit 8 (BOOSTEN) should be set or reset depending on the voltage settings.

**Note:**

When FM+ mode is activated on GPIO pin, the speed configuration of the GPIO (in GPIOx\_OSPEEDR register) is ignored. Program this register only after the AF selection through the GPIOx\_AFRH or GPIOx\_AFRL register.

### 10.2.3  SYSCFG external interrupt configuration register 1   (SYSCFG\_EXTICR1)

Address offset: 0x08

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

**Note:**

Some of the I/O pins mentioned in the above register may not be available on small packages.

### 10.2.5  SYSCFG external interrupt configuration register 3   (SYSCFG\_EXTICR3)

Address offset: 0x10

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

PE[9] pin<br/>0101: PF[9] pin<br/>0110: PG[9] pin</p></td></tr><tr height="158.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 3:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;"> EXTI8[3:0]</span>: EXTI 8 configuration bits</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are written by software to select the source input for the EXTI8 external interrupt. </p><p class="TableIndent">0000: PA[8] pin<br/>0001: PB[8] pin<br/>0010: PC[8] pin<br/>0011: PD[8] pin<br/>0100: PE[8] pin<br/>0101: PF[8] pin<br/>0110: PG[8] pin</p></td></tr></table></div>


**Note:**

Some of the I/O pins mentioned in the above register may not be available on small packages.

### 10.2.6  SYSCFG external interrupt configuration register 4   (SYSCFG\_EXTICR4)

Address offset: 0x14

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

### 10.2.7 SYSCFG CCM SRAM control and status register (SYSCFG\_SCSR)

Address offset: 0x18

System reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="100.40px"/><col width="436.57px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">CCMBSY:</span> CCM SRAM busy by erase operation</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No CCM SRAM erase operation is on going.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: CCM SRAM erase operation is on going.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">CCMER:</span> CCM SRAM Erase </p><p class="TableIndent">Setting this bit starts a hardware CCM SRAM erase operation. This bit is automatically cleared at the end of the CCM SRAM erase operation. </p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is write-protected: setting this bit is possible only after the correct key sequence is written in the SYSCFG_SKR register. </p></td></tr></table></div>


### 10.2.8 SYSCFG configuration register 2 (SYSCFG\_CFGR2)

Address offset: 0x1C

System reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="100.40px"/><col width="436.57px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">SPF</span>: SRAM1 and CCM SRAM parity error flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when an SRAM1 or CCM SRAM parity error is detected. It is cleared by software by writing ‘1’.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No parity error detected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Parity error detected</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value</p></td></tr><tr height="130.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ECCL</span>: ECC Lock</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software and cleared only by a system reset. It can be used to enable and lock the Flash ECC error connection to TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: ECC error disconnected from TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: ECC error connected to TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1. </p></td></tr><tr height="160.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PVDL</span>: PVD lock enable bit</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software and cleared only by a system reset. It can be used to enable and lock the PVD connection to TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1., as well as the PVDE and PLS[2:0] in the PWR_CR2 register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: PVD interrupt disconnected from TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1. PVDE and PLS[2:0] bits can be programmed by the application. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: PVD interrupt connected to TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1, PVDE and PLS[2:0] bits are read only. </p></td></tr><tr height="130.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SPL</span>: SRAM1 and CCM SRAM parity lock bit</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software and cleared only by a system reset. It can be used to enable and lock the SRAM1 or CCM SRAM parity error signal connection to TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: CCM SRAM parity error signal disconnected from TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: CCM SRAM parity error signal connected to TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1.</p></td></tr><tr height="130.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">CLL:</span> Cortex<sup>®</sup>-M4 LOCKUP (Hardfault) output enable bit </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software and cleared only by a system reset. It can be used to enable and lock the connection of <span data-keyref="core">Cortex®-M4 with FPU</span><sup></sup> LOCKUP (Hardfault) output to TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Cortex<sup>®</sup>-M4 LOCKUP output disconnected from TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Cortex<sup>®</sup>-M4 LOCKUP output connected to TIM1/8/15/16/17/20 break input and hrtim_sys_flt input of HRTIM1.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 10.2.9 SYSCFG CCM SRAM write protection register (SYSCFG\_SWPR)

Address offset: 0x20

System reset value: 0x0000 0000

### 10.2.10 SYSCFG CCM SRAM key register (SYSCFG\_SKR)

Address offset: 0x24

System reset value: 0x0000 0000

Write "0xCA” into Key[7:0] </p><p class="TableIndent">2. Write "0x53” into Key[7:0] </p><p class="TableIndent">Writing a wrong key reactivates the write protection.</p></td></tr></table></div>
